Also we had a chat with an e& leader (Nevena) - https://www.linkedin.com/in/nevena-stefanovic-08aa7b3/

Below are the notes from that conversation

AI maturity for e& Leaders:
On a scale of 1-5 most e& leaders are around 2/3 ( 2 = Use GPT proactively, Know how to prompt, 3= They have used other AI tools like MS Copilot as well)
Mostly use GPT like Google search; limited prompting
Use cases have been identified for all business units across the organization
Revenue/commercial functions are more advanced AI users (e.g., voice-to-text, next-best-offer); support functions lag with idea-to-execution gap.
Barriers to AI adoption for e& Leaders:
Time constraints for learning while doing day jobs
Paradigm shift needed: from IT-delivered tools to self-serve AI by every function
Security/compliance uncertainty; lack of clear whitelist/blacklist slows experimentation
Perception among some execs that hands-on building Agents or custom GPTs is “not for leadership.”


Nevena’s perspective & AI usage pattern:
Has an Engineering background and a heavy GPT user with established principles; Uses MS Copilot as well (but Copilot quality lags).
Goal: AI “fluency” (think in AI), offload work to agents like a 10-person team.
Example: Produced board-ready Serbia market share/benchmark analysis in ~15 minutes via GPT with source verification; contrasted with typical 10-day manual effort

Inputs for the e& AI Leaders Accelerator:
Include custom GPTs and agent-building
Tooling stance: PowerUp should teach best-in-class tools even if not yet IT-approved
Tailor to leaders’ use cases: research, analysis, benchmarking, competitive scans
Leverage Nevena as internal champion during training to model outcomes and address skepticism

On Friday we had a chat with another e& leader to get inputs for the AI leaders program. We had a chat with https://www.linkedin.com/in/renatoleitemonteiro/

Renato’s Background & Current Role

Legal team member at e& group focusing on AI regulation, privacy, data protection, intellectual property
Part of AI Governance Committee with cross-functional representation (legal, cybersecurity, risk, strategy, IT)
Responsible for regulatory risk assessments of all AI use cases
Previous experience working with Elon Musk for several years
Renato’s AI Use and Areas of Discontent

Does not use Microsoft AI tools at e& since he finds them to be very poor vs other AI tools
He uses - Claude (primary preference for coding - he was a coder earlier), ChatGPT (mainly for search), Perplexity
Even at e& he used a VM to access apps as web versions since he insisted on getting a Mac
Found existing framework inadequate when joining AI Governance Committee
200+ use cases pending approval with basic checkbox compliance
No proper legal expertise in risk assessment process

Redesigned the entire framework from ground up - this version is not yet public. John will help us get this.
Gap between leadership promises and ground reality
Described disconnect as “Grand Canyon” between public statements and actual capabilities
Frustrated by marketing promises vs. implementation reality

Program Feedback & Recommendations

Strong support for hands-on approach over theoretical training
Emphasized need for regulatory risk assessment module
Suggested “Responsible AI Development” as broader scope
Willing to serve as instructor for regulatory components

Tool selection concerns:
Microsoft Copilot not recommended
Suggested using alternative models available through Microsoft Azure cloud
Offered to help make case to IT for better tools

Program structure suggestions:
Extend from 1.5 days to 2 days to include regulatory component
Focus on practical capability building vs. sales pitch enhancement
Address assessment and risk mitigation processes in the workshop

AI Leaders Program – Inputs for Upcoming Cohorts
Thank you for participating in the AI Leaders session.
 Based on your feedback, we are refining the structure and focus of upcoming cohorts.
 Your responses below will help us design a program that is most relevant and impactful for e& leaders.

1. Preferred Program Structure
For the next cohort, we are planning a two-day program.
Which format would you prefer?
 (Select one)
• ☐ Day 1: Hands-on AI & Agent Building using e&-approved tools
 Day 2: AI Strategy for e& leaders with Hands-on workshops
• ☐ Both days focused on AI Strategy & Leadership
• ☐ Both days focused on hands-on AI tool building
• ☐ Other (please specify):

2. Strategy Deep-Dive Topics
If AI strategy sessions are included, which topics would you like us to go deeper into?
(Select all that apply)
☐ How to measure value and ROI of AI pilots and AI products
☐ Detailed case studies of telecom companies using AI at scale
☐ Framework to prioritize and sequence AI projects for my team or function
☐ How to build AI teams and capabilities for my function
☐ Build vs Buy vs Wait — decision framework for implementing AI solutions in my function
☐ AI operating models (centralized, federated, hybrid)
☐ AI governance, risk, and leadership decision-making
☐ Others (please specify):

3. Priority AI Use Cases at e&
What are the AI use cases you are currently working on, or that are top priorities for your team/function at e&?
(For example: customer service automation, AI voicebots, agent assist for call centers, network optimization, churn prediction, personalized offers, marketing campaign optimization, fraud detection, revenue assurance, knowledge management copilots, employee productivity agents, IT service automation, field-operations optimization, or any other business-critical use case.)
(Open text)

4. What Would Make This Program a Success for You?
(Select up to two)
☐ Prove the value of my selected AI projects to senior leaders and peers
☐ Identify the right AI solutions for my function’s business challenges
☐ Prioritize effectively from my list of AI initiatives
☐ Gain hands-on exposure to modern AI tools and capabilities
☐ Build or scale an AI team for my function
☐ Others (please specify):

5. Any Additional Suggestions
Please share any other suggestions that would help make future AI Leaders sessions more valuable for you.
(Open text) (edited)

@channel For India B2B:
 Given that @Amit Joseph has highlighted multiple times that our pricing in India needs to be competitive (much lower than our current pricing), we can potentially reduce our costs by ~40–70% through the following initiatives:

• Hire Non-FAANG+ SMEs – This can reduce hourly rates by ~50%. For sustained volume, we can also explore onboarding full-time trainers.
 • Adopt a blended delivery model (50% recorded + 50% live) – With sufficient scale/volume, this can significantly lower delivery costs.
 • Increase batch sizes (40–50 learners vs 20–25) – This can reduce per-learner cost by ~50%.
 • Shift infrastructure ownership (AI tools, VMs, etc.) to clients – This can further optimise operational costs.

Please let me know your thoughts on this. @Amit Joseph feel free to add your suggestions or any other concerns for India clients. cc: @Utkarsh Raj

Requirement Sheet


Why the clubbing works:

Kubernetes + Docker + AWS (FT14 + MP153) — Same infra/container skills. Data says “Consolidate with MP153.” Shared core, team-specific labs.                                                                                                                                         
Prompt Engineering (CT77 + CST161) — Identical skill, different team sizes (3 + 50). One course, role-specific applications.               
SQL (FIN122 + CT81) — Both need SQL fundamentals. Split into audit track vs non-developer track.                                           
Python (CT87 + FIN123) — Both need Python basics. Data says “Coordinate with CT87.” Branch into analytics vs audit labs.                   
Advanced GenAI (CT97 + CT99 + MP146) — All building production GenAI in 2026. Nearly identical curricula. Data says “Consolidate for 25-person cohort.”
SRE & Observability (MP143 + CST158) — Same domain. Data says “Consolidate for economies of scale.” 26 people combined.                    
 ML (CT79) — Only 3 people. Data says “Can include them in AI sessions.” Merge into GenAI or Prompt Eng cohort.                             
 Systems Design (FT16) — Linked to SRE proposals but deeper curriculum. Kept separate as a complementary track. 


  Remaining 6 are standalone — unique skills with no overlap.

image.png Utkarsh Raj  [4:13 PM]
replied to a thread:These are the primary questions we need to ask today. @Aashish Kumar @Tanmaya Kharyal summarising based on what Mahima had sent.

The core problem — In your own words, what problem does this system need to solve? Was there a specific moment that triggered this project?
Employee or customer? — Is this about understanding how your employees feel, how your customers feel, or both?
What is People Forum? — Is it a discussion board, a survey tool, or a feedback form? Who uses it and how often?
What is the feedback application? — Do you know its name? Who manages it — is there an IT or HR contact we should speak to?
What does one feedback entry look like? — Does it have ratings, free text, department tags, or a mix? Is it anonymous?
Are there existing categories in the data? — Or is everything free text that we’d need to tag ourselves?
What should a tagged output look like? — Do you want sentiment labels (Positive/Negative), a numeric score, topic themes, or all three?
What should the dashboard show first? — An org-wide score, a department ranking, top recurring issues, or a trend over time?
Who uses the dashboard and in what format? — Just you, or also P&C leadership and department heads? Web app, Power BI, Teams card?
What triggers an alert? — A score dropping below a threshold, a sudden drop, a keyword spike, or a volume surge?
Who receives alerts and how? — Email, Teams channel — and are there topics that should always alert regardless of thresholds?
Can you share a data sample by Thursday? — Even 20–30 anonymised rows. If approvals are needed, who do we go through?
What languages is the data in? — English, Arabic, or mixed?
Roughly how much data exists? — Hundreds of entries, thousands, more?
What does success look like at the demo? — What would make you say “yes, this is exactly what I needed”?
Thanks

Anshuman Bapna  [11:39 AM]
Notes from e&PPF leadership acceleratorWill modify this after our debrief w Nevena next week.

Context: Ryan and I led a 1.5 day workshop for leaders of e&PPF, a large telco in Central Europe. The 15 participants included CEOs of group companies in 4 countries, the group CEO, and sr folks from the group HQ. This is the deck we presented to them - https://acceler-ruby.vercel.app/deck/eppf

# Key takeaways

Went really well. Ryan and I managed to a) connect with the CEOs as peers b) create a high quality conversation amongst them and c) create obvious opportunities for follow-on work. We felt confident we can do this over and over again.
The 1.5 day format worked well. The first day had a natural rhythm of learning, and it surfaced the topics that the leaders cared most about, while the second day ended up being about deciding/taking calls. I can imagine a 2-day version as well that has an actual build session.
Agility in thinking and experience leading companies matters - it showed up in our responses to their questions, and in refactoring Day 2 to take into account what transpired on Day 1. There's also no way this could happen online. Together, this is worrisome because that means it's unscalable for now (max 30-40 a year?). And that means that these are worth doing only if the follow-on business is at least $100K per accelerator.
I never sensed an opportunity to build agents for them. This might change as we do next level leadership, but a) our positioning is not as implementers b) our current format doesn't showcase experts well enough to create credibility on implementation and c) while leadership accelerators build trust, they also catch leaders at an earlier stage in their cycle where scaled implementation may be months away. The last one is particularly important.  Every leader knows of 2-3 AI agents that their teams have built internally - so they think it's easy - but they haven't encountered the pain of deployment, drift etc. I don't know how to address this right now.
Smaller stuff:
  - obviously, the podcast that Madan built as pre-read was a HIT
  - the IK case studies, and especially the demos landed really well
  - PRIME was a hit, we should include it in the main deck
  - Build/buy/breathe was a hit
# Key things to fix

Figure out a better way to showcase experts. My sense is to bring them in for deep dives - on data security, on tokenomics - rather than keeping it more open-ended and fireside-chat style.
Keep a high quality build session in our back pocket (like ANSR). Use it to talk about the real-world challenges of deployment (and thus open up a subtle pitch for our agentic co-building services).
Figure out a way to involve IT. They feel threatened by this agentic world, they're often the bottleneck for business functions, they're the procurement bottleneck for AI, and they would find training like this pointless for themselves. One idea could be to have them present in a session.
Have a solid module on tokenomics


# A reflection on the enterprise environment

Enterprises need two speeds - one, in a sandbox, to procure quickly and experiment fast and another, safer process for savvy vendor evaluation, safe rollouts etc.
IT is going to be a bottleneck every single time
There's real worry about the people & culture impact of AI - we should lean into that. The org, incl the leadership, will sort themselves into high-agency/growth-mindset folks and the rest. The org will have to reorient it's structure around pairing these folks with agents and let the rest of the folks go.
Leaders will always do some posturing - "we already do this" - especially in front of their peers.
I sensed a constant confusion between Horizon 1, 2, 3 of AI amongst leaders. A lot of talking past each other happens when one is subconsciously talking about one horizon while the other person is talking about another. Horizon 1 is the opportunity here & now to improve things (process re-engg, claude's latest features), Horizon 2 is reorganizing the company and it's span of businesses around the AI opportunity (layoffs, new businesses to get into) and Horizon 3 is the existential (Mythos, geopolitics of AI).
With AI, simple process improvement will have a very short shelf life. Will need overhaul/reimagine every time. If nothing else, markets and competitors will force their hand eventually and the longer the org takes to recognize that, the more painful it will be.


# Next steps

Prep for in-country accelerators. From the feedback, it looks like we might need to get a bit more operational there (expert talks?). So far, Serbia is confirmed (June 3-4th), Bulgaria dates are being decided and we'll do our intake call w Hungary next week. We should close these + get Cetin Intl (Jason King) and Slovakia (Igor Toth) to bring us in as well.
Empanel a different set of experts
We should attempt to sign up Balesh/e&PPF as our "design partner" for the agentic building services.
today is off

topics we cover in the EM leadership sessions:
[10:51 AM]We will conduct 6 Leadership Sessions - Workshops on question patterns to understand what recruiters are looking for when they ask questions related to the below categories:
• Leadership & People Management
Master hiring top talent, coaching growth, giving tough feedback, and fostering a psychologically safe, high-trust team.
• Project & Program Management
Plan, prioritise, and ship complex projects: set clear goals, manage scope and risk, coordinate teams, and own results.
• Technical Leadership
Make sound architecture calls, scale systems, balance tech debt vs. innovation, and enforce code-quality and reliability standards.
• Cross-Functional Collaboration
Influence without authority: align with product, design, legal, and execs, negotiate resources, and resolve inter-team conflicts.
• Roadmap & Strategic Thinking
Define vision, build data-driven roadmaps, weigh build-vs-buy, balance short-term delivery with long-term architecture, and tie work to business impact.
• Culture & Team Building
Create an inclusive, engaged, resilient team: drive diversity, maintain morale in tough times, ensure psychological safety, and groom future leaders.[10:54 AM]All these 6 sessions are 2-3 hours

AI Leaders Program – Inputs for Upcoming Cohorts
Thank you for participating in the AI Leaders session.
 Based on your feedback, we are refining the structure and focus of upcoming cohorts.
 Your responses below will help us design a program that is most relevant and impactful for e& leaders.

1. Preferred Program Structure
For the next cohort, we are planning a two-day program.
Which format would you prefer?
 (Select one)
• ☐ Day 1: Hands-on AI & Agent Building using e&-approved tools
 Day 2: AI Strategy for e& leaders with Hands-on workshops
• ☐ Both days focused on AI Strategy & Leadership
• ☐ Both days focused on hands-on AI tool building
• ☐ Other (please specify):

2. Strategy Deep-Dive Topics
If AI strategy sessions are included, which topics would you like us to go deeper into?
(Select all that apply)
☐ How to measure value and ROI of AI pilots and AI products
☐ Detailed case studies of telecom companies using AI at scale
☐ Framework to prioritize and sequence AI projects for my team or function
☐ How to build AI teams and capabilities for my function
☐ Build vs Buy vs Wait — decision framework for implementing AI solutions in my function
☐ AI operating models (centralized, federated, hybrid)
☐ AI governance, risk, and leadership decision-making
☐ Others (please specify):

3. Priority AI Use Cases at e&
What are the AI use cases you are currently working on, or that are top priorities for your team/function at e&?
(For example: customer service automation, AI voicebots, agent assist for call centers, network optimization, churn prediction, personalized offers, marketing campaign optimization, fraud detection, revenue assurance, knowledge management copilots, employee productivity agents, IT service automation, field-operations optimization, or any other business-critical use case.)
(Open text)

4. What Would Make This Program a Success for You?
(Select up to two)
☐ Prove the value of my selected AI projects to senior leaders and peers
☐ Identify the right AI solutions for my function’s business challenges
☐ Prioritize effectively from my list of AI initiatives
☐ Gain hands-on exposure to modern AI tools and capabilities
☐ Build or scale an AI team for my function
☐ Others (please specify):

5. Any Additional Suggestions
Please share any other suggestions that would help make future AI Leaders sessions more valuable for you.
(Open text) (edited)

@channel For India B2B:
 Given that @Amit Joseph has highlighted multiple times that our pricing in India needs to be competitive (much lower than our current pricing), we can potentially reduce our costs by ~40–70% through the following initiatives:

• Hire Non-FAANG+ SMEs – This can reduce hourly rates by ~50%. For sustained volume, we can also explore onboarding full-time trainers.
 • Adopt a blended delivery model (50% recorded + 50% live) – With sufficient scale/volume, this can significantly lower delivery costs.
 • Increase batch sizes (40–50 learners vs 20–25) – This can reduce per-learner cost by ~50%.
 • Shift infrastructure ownership (AI tools, VMs, etc.) to clients – This can further optimise operational costs.

Please let me know your thoughts on this. @Amit Joseph feel free to add your suggestions or any other concerns for India clients. cc: @Utkarsh Raj

Requirement Sheet


Why the clubbing works:

Kubernetes + Docker + AWS (FT14 + MP153) — Same infra/container skills. Data says “Consolidate with MP153.” Shared core, team-specific labs.                                                                                                                                         
Prompt Engineering (CT77 + CST161) — Identical skill, different team sizes (3 + 50). One course, role-specific applications.               
SQL (FIN122 + CT81) — Both need SQL fundamentals. Split into audit track vs non-developer track.                                           
Python (CT87 + FIN123) — Both need Python basics. Data says “Coordinate with CT87.” Branch into analytics vs audit labs.                   
Advanced GenAI (CT97 + CT99 + MP146) — All building production GenAI in 2026. Nearly identical curricula. Data says “Consolidate for 25-person cohort.”
SRE & Observability (MP143 + CST158) — Same domain. Data says “Consolidate for economies of scale.” 26 people combined.                    
 ML (CT79) — Only 3 people. Data says “Can include them in AI sessions.” Merge into GenAI or Prompt Eng cohort.                             
 Systems Design (FT16) — Linked to SRE proposals but deeper curriculum. Kept separate as a complementary track. 


  Remaining 6 are standalone — unique skills with no overlap.

image.png Utkarsh Raj  [4:13 PM]
replied to a thread:These are the primary questions we need to ask today. @Aashish Kumar @Tanmaya Kharyal summarising based on what Mahima had sent.

The core problem — In your own words, what problem does this system need to solve? Was there a specific moment that triggered this project?
Employee or customer? — Is this about understanding how your employees feel, how your customers feel, or both?
What is People Forum? — Is it a discussion board, a survey tool, or a feedback form? Who uses it and how often?
What is the feedback application? — Do you know its name? Who manages it — is there an IT or HR contact we should speak to?
What does one feedback entry look like? — Does it have ratings, free text, department tags, or a mix? Is it anonymous?
Are there existing categories in the data? — Or is everything free text that we’d need to tag ourselves?
What should a tagged output look like? — Do you want sentiment labels (Positive/Negative), a numeric score, topic themes, or all three?
What should the dashboard show first? — An org-wide score, a department ranking, top recurring issues, or a trend over time?
Who uses the dashboard and in what format? — Just you, or also P&C leadership and department heads? Web app, Power BI, Teams card?
What triggers an alert? — A score dropping below a threshold, a sudden drop, a keyword spike, or a volume surge?
Who receives alerts and how? — Email, Teams channel — and are there topics that should always alert regardless of thresholds?
Can you share a data sample by Thursday? — Even 20–30 anonymised rows. If approvals are needed, who do we go through?
What languages is the data in? — English, Arabic, or mixed?
Roughly how much data exists? — Hundreds of entries, thousands, more?
What does success look like at the demo? — What would make you say “yes, this is exactly what I needed”?
Thanks

topics we cover in the EM leadership sessions:
[10:51 AM]We will conduct 6 Leadership Sessions - Workshops on question patterns to understand what recruiters are looking for when they ask questions related to the below categories:
• Leadership & People Management
Master hiring top talent, coaching growth, giving tough feedback, and fostering a psychologically safe, high-trust team.
• Project & Program Management
Plan, prioritise, and ship complex projects: set clear goals, manage scope and risk, coordinate teams, and own results.
• Technical Leadership
Make sound architecture calls, scale systems, balance tech debt vs. innovation, and enforce code-quality and reliability standards.
• Cross-Functional Collaboration
Influence without authority: align with product, design, legal, and execs, negotiate resources, and resolve inter-team conflicts.
• Roadmap & Strategic Thinking
Define vision, build data-driven roadmaps, weigh build-vs-buy, balance short-term delivery with long-term architecture, and tie work to business impact.
• Culture & Team Building
Create an inclusive, engaged, resilient team: drive diversity, maintain morale in tough times, ensure psychological safety, and groom future leaders.[10:54 AM]All these 6 sessions are 2-3 hours
